\left\|\hat{B}^{\top}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}(\hat{B}-B)\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\left\|\mathbb{X}B\right\|_{2}\left\|\mathbb{X}(\hat{B}-B)\right\|_{F}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}+{\frac{1}{N}}\left\|\mathbb{X}(\hat{B}-B)\right\|_{F}^{2}.

(S74)

Term II: We have

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{B}^{\top}(\hat{\Sigma}_{X}-\Sigma_{X})B\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|B^{\top}(\hat{\Sigma}_{X}-\Sigma_{X})B\right\|_{2}+\left\|(\hat{B}-B)^{\top}(\hat{\Sigma}_{X}-\Sigma_{X})B\right\|_{2}

(S75)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|B^{\top}(\hat{\Sigma}_{X}-\Sigma_{X})B\right\|_{2}+\left\|\hat{B}-B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\left\|(\hat{\Sigma}_{X}-\Sigma_{X})B\right\|_{2,\infty}

(S76)

using
\left\|A^{\top}B\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|A\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\left\|B\right\|_{2,\infty}
.

Term 3: We have

\left\|(\hat{B}-B)^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|(\hat{B}-B)^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}B\right\|_{2}\leq\gamma_{1}\left\|(\hat{B}-B)^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}

(S77)

since
\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}B=\tilde{T}D\tilde{H}
where
\tilde{T}
and
\tilde{H}
are orthogonal and
D
is diagonal.

Combining these results, we obtain the statement of the lemma.
\hfill\qed

Proof of Lemma
C.2
:

For reference, Lemma
C.1
gives the bound

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{B}^{T}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\hat{B}-B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\left\|\mathbb{X}B\right\|_{2}\sqrt{\lambda}\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}^{1/2}+\left\|B^{\top}(\Sigma_{X}-\hat{\Sigma}_{X})B\right\|_{2}

(S78)

\displaystyle+\gamma_{1}\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}^{1/2}\left(\lambda\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}+\left\|\hat{\Sigma}_{X}-\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{\operatorname{max}}\right)^{1/2}

(S79)

\displaystyle+\lambda\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}+\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\left\|(\Sigma_{X}-\hat{\Sigma}_{X})B\right\|_{2,\infty}.

(S80)

Proof of slow-rate bound
:

Assuming that
\lambda\geq\frac{2}{N}\left\|\mathbb{X}^{\top}E\right\|_{2,\infty}
, then by Theorem 1 and Corollary 1 of
Gaynanova (2020)
we have

\displaystyle{\frac{1}{N}}\left\|\mathbb{X}(\hat{B}-B)\right\|_{F}^{2}\lesssim\lambda\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}},

(S81)

\displaystyle\left\|(\hat{B}-B)^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\right\|_{F}^{2}\lesssim\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\left(\lambda+\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\left\|\hat{\Sigma}_{X}-\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{\operatorname{max}}\right),

(S82)

and

\left\|\hat{B}\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\lesssim\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}.

(S83)

This last equation implies that
\left\|\hat{B}-B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\lesssim\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}
by the triangle inequality.
Applying these bounds to the terms in Lemma
C.1
establishes the slow-rate bound.

Proof of fast-rate bound
:

Assuming that
\lambda\geq\frac{2}{N}\left\|\mathbb{X}^{\top}E\right\|_{2,\infty}
, that
B
has at most
s
nonzero rows, and assuming the group restricted eigenvalue condition on
\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\mathbb{X}
, then by Theorem 2 and Corollary 2 of
Gaynanova (2020)
we have

\displaystyle{\frac{1}{N}}\left\|\mathbb{X}(\hat{B}-B)\right\|_{F}^{2}\lesssim\kappa_{X}s\lambda^{2}

(S84)

\displaystyle\left\|(\hat{B}-B)^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\right\|_{F}^{2}\lesssim\kappa_{X}s\left(1+\kappa_{X}s\left\|\hat{\Sigma}_{X}-\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{\operatorname{max}}\right)\lambda^{2}

(S85)

and

\left\|\hat{B}-B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\lesssim\kappa_{X}s\lambda.

(S86)

Applying these bounds to the terms in Lemma
C.1
establishes the fast-rate bound.
\hfill\qed

Proof of Lemma
C.3
:

From the definition of
E
,
E=\mathbb{Y}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\mathbb{X}B
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}{\mathbb{X}^{\top}E}

\displaystyle={\frac{1}{N}}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{Y}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-{\frac{1}{N}}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{X}B

(S87)

\displaystyle=\hat{\Sigma}_{XY}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\hat{\Sigma}_{X}B

(S88)

\displaystyle=\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{XY}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)+\left(\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}-\hat{\Sigma}_{X}B\right).

(S89)

Now considering the first term in equation (
S89
), by adding and subtracting
\Sigma_{XY}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}
, and subsequently adding and subtracting
\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}
to
\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\Sigma}_{XY}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}

\displaystyle=\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{XY}-\Sigma_{XY}\right)\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}+\Sigma_{XY}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)

(S90)

\displaystyle=\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{XY}-\Sigma_{XY}\right)\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)+\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{XY}-\Sigma_{XY}\right)\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}+\Sigma_{XY}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right).

(S91)

For the second term, we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}-\hat{\Sigma}_{X}B

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{X}\Sigma_{X}^{-1}\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}-\hat{\Sigma}_{X}B

(S92)

\displaystyle=\left(\Sigma_{X}-\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\right)B.

(S93)

Combining these equalities and using the triangle inequality completes the proof.
\hfill\qed

Proof of Lemma
C.5
:

The proof is adapted from Lemma 7 of
Gaynanova (2020)
but considers cross-covariance matrices rather than the covariance matrices.
Let
X_{ij}
denote entry
(i,j)
of
\mathbb{X}
and
Z_{ij}
denote entry
(i,j)
of
\mathbb{Z}
. Then

{\frac{1}{N}}\left(\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{Z}\right)_{kl}={\frac{1}{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}X_{ik}Z_{il},\qquad k=1,\ldots p,\text{ }l=1,\ldots d.

(S94)

Let
\Sigma_{XZ}
be the cross-covariance matrix of
X
and
Z
with
(k,l)
entry equal to
\sigma_{kl}=\mathbb{E}\left[X_{ik}Z_{il}\right]
. Then,
X_{ik}Z_{il}-\sigma_{kl}
are each mean
0
subexponential random variables, since

\left\|X_{ik}Z_{il}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\left\|X_{ik}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\left\|Z_{il}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}=g_{k}h_{l}\leq gh

by Lemma 2.7.7. of
Vershynin (2018)
, and because

\left\|X_{ik}Z_{il}-\sigma_{kl}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\lesssim\left\|X_{ik}Z_{il}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}

by Exercise 2.7.10. of
Vershynin (2018)
. Thus,
\left(\Sigma_{XZ}-{\frac{1}{N}}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{Z}\right)_{kl}
is a sum of independently and identically distributed (i.i.d.) subexponential random variables, since each for fixed
k
and
l
, the
X_{ik}Z_{il}-\sigma_{kl}
are mean
0
subexponential i.i.d. random variables over
i=1,\ldots N
.

By Corollary 2.8.3. of
Vershynin (2018)
, (Berstein’s inequality), for each
k
and
l
, and for every
t>0
,

P\left(\left|\left(\Sigma_{XZ}-{\frac{1}{N}}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{Z}\right)_{kl}\right|\geq t\right)\leq 2\operatorname{exp}\left[-cN\operatorname{min}\left(\frac{t^{2}}{K^{2}},\frac{t}{K}\right)\right],

(S95)

where
K=Cgh
, for absolute constants
c
and
C
. Applying a union bound, we have

P\left(\left\|\Sigma_{XZ}-{\frac{1}{N}}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{Z}\right\|_{\operatorname{max}}\geq t\right)\leq 2dp\operatorname{exp}\left[-cN\operatorname{min}\left(\frac{t^{2}}{K^{2}},\frac{t}{K}\right)\right].

(S96)

When
t\leq K
, we have

P\left(\left\|\Sigma_{XZ}-{\frac{1}{N}}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{Z}\right\|_{\operatorname{max}}\geq t\right)\leq 2dp\operatorname{exp}\left[-cN\operatorname{min}\frac{t^{2}}{K^{2}}\right].

(S97)

since
t^{2}/K^{2}\leq t/K
if and only if
t\leq K
.
Letting the right-hand side of equation (
S97
) be denoted as
\eta
, we solve for
t
in terms of
\eta
to obtain

t=\sqrt{\operatorname{log}\left(2dp\eta^{-1}\right)\frac{K^{2}}{cN}},

(S98)

and so that, for
\eta\in(0,1)
, if
\sqrt{\operatorname{log}\left(2dp\eta^{-1}\right)\frac{K^{2}}{cN}}\leq Cgh
, then with probability at least
1-\eta
we have

\left\|\Sigma_{XZ}-{\frac{1}{N}}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{Z}\right\|_{\operatorname{max}}\lesssim gh\sqrt{\operatorname{log}\left(2dp\eta^{-1}\right)\frac{1}{N}}.

(S99)

\operatorname{log}\left(2dp\eta^{-1}\right)\leq\operatorname{log}(2)+2\operatorname{log}\left(p\eta^{-1}\right)
since
d\leq p
, and because we suppose
\operatorname{log}(p)=o(N)
,
it follows that for fixed
\eta\in(0,1)
, with probability
1-\eta
,

\left\|\Sigma_{XZ}-{\frac{1}{N}}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{Z}\right\|_{\operatorname{max}}\lesssim gh\sqrt{\operatorname{log}\left(p\eta^{-1}\right)\frac{1}{N}}.

(S100)

Using
\left\|A\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\sqrt{d}\left\|A\right\|_{\operatorname{max}}
for any
A\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times d}
completes the proof.
\hfill\qed

Proof of Lemma
C.6
:

To establish

\left\|\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{XY}-\Sigma_{XY}\right)\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\operatorname{max}_{i}(\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{\psi_{2}})\sqrt{\frac{d}{N}\operatorname{log}(p\eta^{-1})},

(S101)

we can use Lemma
C.5
where
X=X
and
Z=\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y
, since
\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}=\Sigma_{X,\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y}
. Then, the sub-Gaussian norms are
g=\operatorname{max}_{i}(\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{\psi_{2}})
and
h=\operatorname{max}_{i}\left(\left\|\left(\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y\right)_{i}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\right)
,
where
\left(\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y\right)_{i}
is the
i
th entry of
\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
. Using Definition
C.1
for
\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y
, we have

h\leq K_{\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y}\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{Y}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}^{1/2}=K_{\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y}.

(S102)

Treating
K_{\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y}
as an absolute constant establishes equation (
S101
).

Establishing

\left\|\left(\Sigma_{X}-\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\right)B\right\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\operatorname{max}_{i}(\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{\psi_{2}})\gamma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d}{N}\operatorname{log}(p\eta^{-1})}

(S103)

follows an identical argument except that we let
Z=B^{\top}X
, so

h\leq K_{B^{\top}X}\left\|B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B\right\|_{2}^{1/2}

in the final step. The identity
\left\|B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B\right\|_{2}=\gamma_{1}^{2}
establishes equation (
S103
).

To deduce that

\left\|\Sigma_{XY}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\right\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{1/2}\gamma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d+\operatorname{log}(\eta^{-1})}{N}},

(S104)

we begin by using
\left\|AB\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\left\|A\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|B\right\|_{2}
and
\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}=I_{d}
to obtain

\left\|\Sigma_{XY}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\underbrace{\left\|\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2,\infty}}_{\text{Term I}}\underbrace{\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\right\|_{2}}_{\text{Term II}}.

(S105)

Considering Term I, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2,\infty}

(S106)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}B\right\|_{2}

(S107)

\displaystyle=\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\right\|_{2,\infty}\gamma_{1}.

(S108)

In the below, we are able to bound Term II without incurring unnecessary factors of
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}
using the results of
Kereta and Klock (2021)
which pertain to precision matrix estimation along subspaces. The main idea is to bound
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\right\|_{2}
in terms of
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right)\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}
, to which the results of
Kereta and Klock (2021)
can be applied. That
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\right\|_{2}
is not simply equal to
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right)\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}
is due to
\Sigma_{Y}
and
\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}
not necessarily commuting with one another. We begin with

\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\right\|_{2}=\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-I\right\|_{2}.

(S109)

Using identity
15
in Section
F
and that both
\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}
and
\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{1/2}
are positive definite along with their inverses,

\text{Term II}=\left\|\left(\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1}\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}-I\right\|_{2}=\left\|\left(\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1}\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}-\left(\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}\right\|_{2}.

(S110)

Using
\left\|A^{1/2}-B^{1/2}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{max}\left(\left\|A^{-1}\right\|_{2},\left\|B^{-1}\right\|_{2}\right)^{1/2}\left\|A-B\right\|_{2}
for positive definite matrices
A
and
B
, we deduce that

Term II

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{max}\left(\left\|\left(\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1}\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{2},\left\|I_{d}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\right)^{1/2}\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1}\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}

(S111)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{max}\left(\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}\right\|_{2},1\right)^{1/2}}_{\text{Term II.I}}\underbrace{\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right)\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}}_{\text{{Term II.I}}}.

(S112)

We bound
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}\right\|_{2}
in Term II.I with

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}-\Sigma_{Y}\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2}

(S113)

\displaystyle=\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}-\Sigma_{Y}\right)\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}+1.

(S114)

We apply a result concerning covariance estimation along subspaces, Lemma 2 of
Kereta and Klock (2021)
, to deduce that for fixed
\eta\in(0,1)
with probability
1-\eta
,

\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}-\Sigma_{Y}\right)\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\operatorname{max}\left(\sqrt{\frac{2d+\operatorname{log}(\eta^{-1})}{N}},\frac{2d+\operatorname{log}(\eta^{-1})}{N}\right).

(S115)

From Definition
C.1
for
\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y
combined with the assumption that
d=o(N)
, we have that in the limit this term is bounded by
1
. Therefore, for fixed
\eta\in(0,1)
, with probability
1-\eta
,
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}\right\|_{2}\lesssim 1
. From this,
\text{Term II.I}\lesssim 1
as well.

Having bounded
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\right\|_{2}
in terms of
\text{Term II.II}=\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right)\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}
, we will bound the latter term. We use Theorem 10 of
Kereta and Klock (2021)
directly, implying that for fixed
\eta\in(0,1)
, if
N\gtrsim(d+\operatorname{log}(\eta^{-1})\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{4}
, then with probability
1-\eta
,

\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right)\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{rank}\left(\Sigma_{Y}\right)+\operatorname{log}(\eta^{-1})}{N}}.

(S116)

By Definition
C.1
for
\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y
,
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim 1
, so that the assumption
d=o(N)
ensures that for fixed
\eta
,
N\gtrsim\left(d+\operatorname{log}(\eta^{-1})\right)\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{4}
eventually.

With our bounds for both Term II.I and Term II.II, we deduce that for fixed
\eta\in(0,1)
, with probability
1-\eta
,

\text{Term II}=\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\right\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d+\operatorname{log}(\eta^{-1})}{N}}.

(S117)

Now having bounded both Term I and Term II, we finally establish that for fixed
\eta\in(0,1)
, if
d=o(N)
, with probability
1-\eta
,

\left\|\Sigma_{XY}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\right\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{1/2}\gamma_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d+\operatorname{log}(\eta^{-1})}{N}},

(S118)

completing the proof of (
S104
).

To show

\left\|\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{XY}-\Sigma_{XY}\right)\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\right\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\left\|\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{XY}-\Sigma_{XY}\right)\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2,\infty},

(S119)

we begin with

\left\|\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{XY}-\Sigma_{XY}\right)\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\left\|\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{XY}-\Sigma_{XY}\right)\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\right\|_{2},

(S120)

using
\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}=I_{d}
and
\left\|AB\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\left\|A\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|B\right\|_{2}
. From equation (
S117
) we obtain that with probability
1-\eta
, the second factor in (
S120
) is bounded by an absolute constant as
d=o(N)
, completing the proof. ∎

Proof of Lemma
C.8
:

That for fixed
\eta\in(0,1)
, if
\operatorname{log}(p)=o(N)
, then with probability
1-\eta
,

\left\|\Sigma_{X}-\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\right\|_{\operatorname{max}}\lesssim\operatorname{max}(\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2})\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{log}\left(p\eta^{-1}\right)}{N}}

(S121)

follows from Lemma 7 of
Gaynanova (2020)
.

That for fixed
\eta\in(0,1)
, if
d=o(N)
, then with probability
1-\eta
,

\left\|B^{\top}(\Sigma_{X}-\hat{\Sigma}_{X})B\right\|_{2}\lesssim\gamma_{1}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{rank}\left(B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}\right)+\operatorname{log}\left(\eta^{-1}\right)}{N}}

(S122)

follows from Lemma 2 of
Kereta and Klock (2021)
in addition to Definition
C.1
applied to
B^{\top}X
, which implies that
\left\|B^{\top}X\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq K_{B^{\top}X}\left\|B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B\right\|_{2}^{1/2}
.
Using
\operatorname{rank}\left(AB\right)\leq\operatorname{min}\left(\operatorname{rank}\left(A\right),\operatorname{rank}\left(B\right)\right)
, we have

\operatorname{rank}\left(B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}\right)=\operatorname{rank}\left(\Sigma_{X}B\right)=\operatorname{rank}\left(\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\leq d,

(S123)

which establishes the desired result.

To show that for fixed
\eta\in(0,1)
, if
d=o(N)
, then with probability
1-\eta
,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\left\|\mathbb{X}B\right\|_{2}\lesssim\gamma_{1},

(S124)

we begin with

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\left\|\mathbb{X}B\right\|_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\left\|B^{\top}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{X}B\right\|_{2}^{1/2}=\left\|B^{\top}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}B\right\|_{2}^{1/2},

(S125)

which holds since
\left\|A\right\|_{2}=\left\|A^{\top}A\right\|_{2}^{1/2}
. Adding and subtracting
B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B
and using the triangle inequality, we obtain that for fixed
\eta\in(0,1)
, with probability
1-\eta
,

\left\|B^{\top}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}B\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B\right\|_{2}+\left\|B^{\top}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{X}-\Sigma_{X}\right)B\right\|_{2}\lesssim\gamma_{1}^{2}.

(S126)

In the last inequality, we have used
d=o(N)
and equation (
S122
) to deduce that
\left\|B^{\top}\left(\hat{\Sigma}_{X}-\Sigma_{X}\right)B\right\|_{2}
becomes smaller that
\gamma_{1}^{2}
eventually. This completes the proof. ∎

Proof of Lemma
C.9
:

By Lemma 6 of
Gaynanova (2020)
, if suffices to show that under the condition
s^{2}\log(p)=o(N)
, that for fixed
\eta
, with probability
1-\eta
, we have
s\left\|\Sigma_{X}-\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\right\|_{\operatorname{max}}\leq(32\kappa)^{-1}
. By the first item of Lemma
C.8
, we then have that for fixed
\eta
with probability
1-\eta
,

\kappa s\left\|\Sigma_{X}-\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\right\|_{\operatorname{max}}\lesssim\kappa s\operatorname{max}_{i}(\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2})\sqrt{\frac{\log\left(p\eta^{-1}\right)}{N}}.

(S127)

It therefore suffices that
\operatorname{max}_{i}(\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{4})\kappa^{2}s^{2}\log\left(p\right)=o(N)
, since then with probability
1-\eta
,
\kappa s\left\|\Sigma_{X}-\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\right\|_{\operatorname{max}}
is arbitrarily small. But this is the assumed condition, so the proof is complete. ∎

Proof of Lemma
C.10
:

Proof of slow-rate bound
:

Under the slow-rate assumptions of Corollary
C.1
and by Corollary 1 in
Gaynanova (2020)
, for fixed
\eta
, with probability
1-\eta
, we have

\left\|B-\hat{B}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\left(\lambda+\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\left\|\hat{\Sigma}_{X}-\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{\operatorname{max}}\right).

(S128)

Then

\lambda\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{1/2}\left(\frac{d}{N}\log\left(p\eta^{-1}\right)\right)^{1/2},

(S129)

and by Lemma
C.8
,

\left\|\Sigma_{X}-\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\right\|_{\operatorname{max}}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left(\frac{d}{N}\log\left(p\eta^{-1}\right)\right)^{1/2}.

(S130)

The last two equations bound
\left\|B-\hat{B}\right\|_{2}
, and since
\left\|\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{2,\infty},\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\geq 1
, the proof of the slow-rate bound is complete.

Proof of fast-rate bound
:

Under the slow-rate assumptions of Corollary
C.1
and by Theorem 2 in
Gaynanova (2020)
, for fixed
\eta
, with probability
1-\eta
, we have

\left\|B-\hat{B}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\kappa s^{1/2}\lambda.

(S131)

Again using
\lambda\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{1/2}\left(\frac{d}{N}\log\left(p\eta^{-1}\right)\right)^{1/2}
, the result is shown. ∎

Proof of Lemma
C.11
:

The proof of the first statement follows from Lemma 2 of
Kereta and Klock (2021)
and Definition
C.1
applied to
Y
.

To show the bound on
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}-\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}
, we begin by using identity
13
in Section
F
applied to
\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}
and
\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1}
. We have

\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}-\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{max}\left(\left\|\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2},\left\|\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}\right\|_{2}\right)^{1/2}\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}-\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}.

(S132)

To bound
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}-\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}
, we use Corollary 11 of
Kereta and Klock (2021)
: if

\left(\operatorname{rank}\left(\Sigma_{Y}\right)+\log\left(\eta^{-1}\right)\right)\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}Y\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{4}\lesssim N

eventually, then with probability
1-\eta
,

\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}-\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}Y\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{rank}\left(\Sigma_{Y}\right)+\log\left(\eta^{-1}\right)}{N}}.

(S133)

The variance proxy condition on
\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}Y
(Definition
C.1
) implies that
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y\right\|_{\psi_{2}}
is bounded by an absolute constant. The condition
d=o(N)
then ensures
\left(\operatorname{rank}\left(\Sigma_{Y}\right)+\log\left(\eta^{-1}\right)\right)\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}Y\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{4}\lesssim N
eventually. The variance proxy condition on
\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}Y
implies
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}Y\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}
.

To bound
\left\|\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}\right\|_{2}
, we use the triangle inequality to obtain
\left\|\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}-\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2}
. Then, using the first statement of the lemma and with the additional assumption that
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2}^{2}d=o(N)
, we have with probability
1-\eta
,

\left\|\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2}.

(S134)

Combining these results together establishes the statement of the lemma and the proof is complete. ∎

Proof of Theorems
C.2
and
C.3
:

Proof of bounds for
\theta
:

By definition, we have that

\displaystyle\theta_{k}=B\tilde{\eta}_{k}\gamma_{k}^{-1}

(S135)

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{k}=\hat{B}\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{-1}.

(S136)

We bound
\left\|\theta_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}
by bounding all three of
\left\|B-\hat{B}\right\|_{2}
,
\left\|\tilde{\eta}_{k}-\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}\right\|_{2}
, and
\left|\gamma_{k}^{-1}-\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{-1}\right|
.
For ease of notation, we denote
\tilde{\eta}_{k}
by
v
. We begin with

\displaystyle\left\|\theta_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|Bv\gamma_{k}^{-1}-Bv\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{-1}\right\|_{2}+\left\|Bv\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{-1}-\hat{B}\hat{v}\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{-1}\right\|_{2}

(S137)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\gamma_{k}^{-1}-\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{-1}\right|\left\|Bv\right\|_{2}+\left|\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{-1}\right|\left\|Bv-\hat{B}\hat{v}\right\|_{2}.

(S138)

Examining
\left\|Bv-\hat{B}\hat{v}\right\|_{2}
on the right-hand side of (
S138
):

\displaystyle\left\|Bv-\hat{B}\hat{v}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|Bv-B\hat{v}\right\|_{2}+\left\|B\hat{v}-\hat{B}\hat{v}\right\|_{2}

(S139)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|B\right\|_{2}\left\|v-\hat{v}\right\|_{2}+\left\|B-\hat{B}\right\|_{2}\left\|\hat{v}\right\|_{2}.

(S140)

Using (
S138
) and since
\left\|\hat{v}\right\|_{2}=1
, we have

\left\|\theta_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}\leq\left|\gamma_{k}^{-1}-\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{-1}\right|\left\|Bv\right\|_{2}+\left|\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{-1}\right|\left(\left\|B\right\|_{2}\left\|v-\hat{v}\right\|_{2}+\left\|B-\hat{B}\right\|_{2}\right).

(S141)

where we note that we now have bounded
\left\|\theta_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}
in terms of
\left\|B-\hat{B}\right\|_{2}
,
\left\|\tilde{\eta}_{k}-\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}\right\|_{2}
, and
\left|\gamma_{k}^{-1}-\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{-1}\right|
.
To bound
\left|\gamma_{k}^{-1}-\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{-1}\right|
, we can use identity
16
from Section
F
, giving us that

\left|\gamma_{k}^{-1}-\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{-1}\right|\leq\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k},\hat{\gamma}_{k}\right)^{-3}\left|\gamma_{k}^{2}-\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{2}\right|.

(S142)

Bounding the two factors in equation (
S142
) amounts to establishing that
\gamma_{k}
is close to
\hat{\gamma}_{k}
. For this, we apply Weyl’s inequality (
Bhatia (2013)
Corollary III.2.6.) to obtain

\left|\gamma_{k}^{2}-\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{2}\right|\leq\left\|B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B-\hat{B}^{\top}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\hat{B}\right\|_{2}.

(S143)

since
\gamma_{k}^{2}
is the
k
th eigenvalue of
B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B
, and
\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{2}
is the
k
th eigenvalue of
\hat{B}^{\top}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\hat{B}
. We then deduce that

\gamma_{k}^{2}-\left\|B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B-\hat{B}^{\top}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\hat{B}\right\|_{2}\leq\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{2}.

(S144)

From equation (
S144
) we establish that
\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{k}^{2}\leq\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{2}
by using

\left\|B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B-\hat{B}^{\top}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\hat{B}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{k}^{2},

(S145)

which holds asymptotically in both the fast and slow rate cases under our assumption that the
\gamma_{k}
are bounded from below and from Corollary
C.1
. From
\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{k}^{2}\leq\hat{\gamma}_{k}^{2}
we also deduce

\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k},\hat{\gamma}_{k}\right)^{-2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\gamma_{k}^{2}},

(S146)

and additionally that

\frac{1}{\hat{\gamma}_{k}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\gamma_{k}}.

(S147)

We now use our results thus far regarding
\gamma_{k}
and
\hat{\gamma_{k}}
to obtain a simplified equation (
S141
):

\left\|\theta_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\gamma_{k}^{3}}\left\|B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B-\hat{B}^{\top}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\hat{B}\right\|_{2}\left\|Bv\right\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{k}}\left(\left\|B\right\|_{2}\left\|v-\hat{v}\right\|_{2}+\left\|B-\hat{B}\right\|_{2}\right)

(S148)

To bound
\left\|v-\hat{v}\right\|_{2}
, we can use the Davis-Kahan theorem (Corollary 3 of
Yu et al. (2015)
). Assuming that
\tilde{\eta_{k}}^{\top}\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}\geq 0
, then

\left\|\tilde{\eta_{k}}-\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{2^{3/2}\left\|B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B-\hat{B}^{\top}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\hat{B}\right\|_{2}}{\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}^{2}-\gamma_{k}^{2},\gamma_{k}^{2}-\gamma_{k+1}^{2}\right)},

(S149)

because
\tilde{\eta_{k}}
is the
k
th eigenvector of
B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B
, and
\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}
is the
k
th eigenvector of
\hat{B}^{\top}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\hat{B}
. From this and equation (
S148
), we obtain

\left\|\theta_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\gamma_{k}^{2}}\left\|B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B-\hat{B}^{\top}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\hat{B}\right\|_{2}\left\|\theta_{k}\right\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{k}}\left(\left\|B\right\|_{2}\frac{\left\|B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B-\hat{B}^{\top}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\hat{B}\right\|_{2}}{\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}^{2}-\gamma_{k}^{2},\gamma_{k}^{2}-\gamma_{k+1}^{2}\right)}+\left\|B-\hat{B}\right\|_{2}\right),

(S150)

where we have also used the definition of
\theta_{k}
,
\theta_{k}=Bv\gamma_{k}^{-1}
.
Rearranging this expression, we have

\left\|\theta_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left(\frac{\left\|\theta_{k}\right\|_{2}}{\gamma_{k}^{2}}+\frac{\left\|B\right\|_{2}}{\gamma_{k}\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}^{2}-\gamma_{k}^{2},\gamma_{k}^{2}-\gamma_{k+1}^{2}\right)}\right)\left\|B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B-\hat{B}^{\top}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\hat{B}\right\|_{2}+\frac{\left\|B-\hat{B}\right\|_{2}}{\gamma_{k}}.

(S151)

Now we use our bounds for
\left\|B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B-\hat{B}^{\top}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\hat{B}\right\|_{2}
and
\left\|B-\hat{B}\right\|_{2}
depending on if we are in the slow or fast rate case. In the slow rate case, we also use

\left\|\theta_{k}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\tilde{\theta_{k}}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\left\|\tilde{\theta_{k}}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2},

(S152)

where we have used the standard result of classical CCA that
\theta_{k}=\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\tilde{\theta_{k}}
for some unit vector
\tilde{\theta_{k}}

(
Uurtio et al., 2018
)
.

The proofs for both the fast and slow rate then follow directly from Lemma
C.10
, Theorem
C.1
, and rearranging of terms. This completes the proof of the bounds on
\left\|\theta_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}
.

Proof of bounds for
\eta
:

By definition, we have

\displaystyle\eta_{k}=\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\tilde{\eta_{k}},

(S153)

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}_{k}=\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}.

(S154)

We bound
\left\|\eta_{k}-\hat{\eta}_{k}\right\|_{2}
with the triangle inequality:

\displaystyle\left\|\eta_{k}-\hat{\eta}_{k}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\tilde{\eta_{k}}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}-\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}\right\|_{2}

(S155)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\left\|\tilde{\eta_{k}}-\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}-\hat{\Sigma}_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\left\|\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}\right\|_{2}.

(S156)

To simplify this expression, we can use
\left\|\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}\right\|_{2}=1
, the Davis-Kahan Theorem for
\left\|\tilde{\eta_{k}}-\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}\right\|_{2}
as in the proof for the
\theta
bounds, and the second statement of Lemma
C.11
.
For clarity, we state the assumptions required for these results:
d=o(N)
,
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2}^{2}d=o(N)
, the variance proxy condition (Definition
C.1
) for
Y
and
\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}Y
, and
\tilde{\eta_{k}}^{\top}\hat{\tilde{\eta_{k}}}\geq 0
for
k=1,\ldots K
. Then, for
\eta\in(0,1)
, we have

\left\|\eta_{k}-\hat{\eta}_{k}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\left[\frac{\left\|\hat{B}^{T}\hat{\Sigma}_{X}\hat{B}-B^{\top}\Sigma_{X}B\right\|_{2}}{\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}^{2}-\gamma_{k}^{2},\gamma_{k}^{2}-\gamma_{k+1}^{2}\right)}+\left\|\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2}^{1/2}\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\sqrt{\frac{d\log\left(\eta^{-1}\right)}{N}}\right].

(S157)

Now we apply Corollary
C.1
to equation (
S157
) under the fast and slow rate assumptions. In the slow rate case, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\eta_{k}-\hat{\eta}_{k}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\Bigg[\frac{\gamma_{1}\left\|\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{1/2}\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}}{\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}^{2}-\gamma_{k}^{2},\gamma_{k}^{2}-\gamma_{k+1}^{2}\right)}\left(\frac{d}{N}\log\left(p\eta^{-1}\right)\right)^{1/4}

\displaystyle+\left\|\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2}^{1/2}\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\left(\frac{d\log\left(\eta^{-1}\right)}{N}\right)^{1/2}\Bigg].

Factoring out
\left(\frac{d}{N}\right)^{1/4}
we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\eta_{k}-\hat{\eta}_{k}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\left(\frac{d}{N}\right)^{1/4}\Bigg[\frac{\gamma_{1}\left\|\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{1/2}\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}}{\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}^{2}-\gamma_{k}^{2},\gamma_{k}^{2}-\gamma_{k+1}^{2}\right)}\log\left(p\eta^{-1}\right)^{1/4}

\displaystyle+\left\|\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2}^{1/2}\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\log\left(\eta^{-1}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{d}{N}\right)^{1/4}\Bigg].

In the bracketed expression we are able to combine the first and second terms, since we assume
\gamma_{1}
is bounded from below, using the additional assumption that
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}d=o(N)
, and because the other terms in the first term are greater than or equal to
1
. Then, with probability
1-\eta
,

\left\|\eta_{k}-\hat{\eta}_{k}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\gamma_{1}\left\|\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{1/2}\left\|B\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}}{\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}^{2}-\gamma_{k}^{2},\gamma_{k}^{2}-\gamma_{k+1}^{2}\right)}\left(\frac{d\log\left(p\eta^{-1}\right)}{N}\right)^{1/4},

(S158)

completing the proof of the slow-rate bound for
\eta
.

In the fast-rate case, under the fast-rate bound assumptions of Corollary
C.1
and applying Corollary
C.1
to equation (
S157
), we establish

\left\|\eta_{k}-\hat{\eta}_{k}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\operatorname{max}\left(\frac{\gamma_{1}\left\|\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{1/2}s^{1/2}\kappa^{1/2}}{\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}^{2}-\gamma_{k}^{2},\gamma_{k}^{2}-\gamma_{k+1}^{2}\right)},\left\|\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2}^{1/2}\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}^{1/2}\right)\left(\frac{d\log\left(p\eta^{-1}\right)}{N}\right)^{1/2},

(S159)

completing the proof of the fast-rate bound for
\eta
. ∎

Appendix D
Asymmetric Sparse-Functional CCA: Proof of Theorem
3.2

In this section, we prove Theorem
3.2
. Recall that we use Assumption
1.1
where the
\{\phi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
are the principal components of
\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y
, and without loss of generality suppose that
I=\left\{1,\ldots{d^{(\text{corr})}}\right\}
. We suppose that
d
, the number of principal components we use in practice, satisfies
d\geq{d^{(\text{corr})}}
. Define
Y_{j}\equiv\llangle\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y,\phi_{j}\rrangle
for
j=1,\ldots d
, so that the
\{Y_{j}\}
are random variables with
\operatorname{Var}\left(Y_{j}\right)\equiv\omega_{j}
. Let
Y=(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{d})^{\top}
. Then, by Theorem
1.1
the canonical pairs
\{(\psi_{k},\theta_{k})\}
can be computed by solving a
{d^{(\text{corr})}}
-dimensional multivariate CCA problem between
X
and
Y^{{d^{(\text{corr})}}}\equiv(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{d^{(\text{corr})}})^{\top}
. It is straightforward to show that thanks to Assumption
1.1
and the properties of the singular value decomposition, we can equivalently solve a
d
-dimensional multivariate CCA problem between
X
and
Y
:

\underset{\operatorname{Var}\left(\eta_{1}^{\top}Y\right)=\operatorname{Var}\left(\theta_{1}^{\top}X\right)=1}{\operatorname{maximize}\ }\operatorname{Corr}^{2}\left(\eta_{1}^{\top}Y,\theta_{1}^{\top}X\right),

(S160)

with subsequent canonical pairs defined analogously, where
Y
has replaced
Y^{{d^{(\text{corr})}}}
. We denote with
K=\operatorname{max}\left\{i\in\{1,\ldots{d^{(\text{corr})}}\}:\gamma_{i}>0\right\}
the number of nontrivial canonical vectors in this problem, and to simplify the notation, we use the conventions
\gamma_{K+1}^{2}=-\infty
and
\gamma_{0}^{2}=\infty
. Here,
\eta_{k}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
\theta_{k}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
for
k=1,\ldots K
. The canonical pairs are then given by
\left(\psi_{k}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\phi_{j}\eta_{kj},\theta_{k}\right)
, where
\eta_{kj}=0
for
j>{d^{(\text{corr})}}
. Recall that
\mathcal{C}\equiv\mathbb{E}\left[\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y\otimes\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y\right]
admits the expansion
\mathcal{C}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\omega_{j}\phi_{j}\otimes\phi_{j}
, where
\{\phi_{j}\}
are the eigenfunctions of
\mathcal{C}
with associated eigenvalues
\{\omega_{j}\}
. We let
\gamma_{1}^{2}\ldots\gamma_{K}^{2}
denote the squared canonical correlations attained by the pairs
\left(\psi_{1},\theta_{1}\right),\ldots\left(\psi_{K},\theta_{K}\right)
.

We denote by
\hat{\psi}_{k}
and
\hat{\theta}_{k}
the canonical vectors estimated using our proposed Algorithm
1
. In practice, we are given a sample of
N
independent pairs

(y_{i},X_{i}),\quad i=1,\ldots,N,

(S161)

where each pair
(y_{i},X_{i})
is an independent observation of the pair
(y,X)
. Here, the functions
\{y_{i}\}
are assumed to be fully observed on
\mathcal{T}
. We denote
\tau\equiv|\mathcal{T}|
, the length of the time interval of the functional data. We store the observations
\{X_{i}\}
in a matrix
\mathbb{X}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N\times p}
.

In Algorithm
1
, we estimate
\mu
using the sample Fréchet mean, denoted as
\hat{\mu}
, and estimate the eigenfunctions
\{\phi_{j}\}
using
\hat{\phi}_{j}
, which are the eigenfunctions of the sample covariance function
\hat{\mathcal{C}}\equiv\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{i}\otimes\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{i}
. Hence, the functional data can be represented using the vector
Z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, where its
j
th element is

Z_{j}\equiv\llangle\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{1},\hat{\phi}_{j}\rrangle_{\hat{\mu}}.

(S162)

We note that in the definition of
Z
, both
\hat{\mu}
and
\hat{\phi}_{j}
depend on
y_{1}
since their estimation depends on the full sample
y_{1},\ldots y_{N}
. We also note that the distribution of
Z
depends on the sample size
N
. In practice, we solve the following problem for the first pair of canonical variables:

\underset{\operatorname{Var}\left(a_{1}^{\top}Z\right)=\operatorname{Var}\left(b_{1}^{\top}X\right)=1}{\operatorname{maximize}\ }\operatorname{Corr}^{2}\left(a_{1}^{\top}Z,b_{1}^{\top}X\right).

(S163)

The subsequent canonical pairs can be defined analogously. We denote the solutions to these problems as
\left(a_{1},b_{1}\right),\ldots\left(a_{K},b_{K}\right)
. We let
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{2}\ldots\tilde{\gamma}_{K}^{2}
denote the squared canonical correlations attained by the pairs
\left(a_{1},b_{1}\right),\ldots\left(a_{K},b_{K}\right)
.
We expect that, under appropriate assumptions,
{a_{k}}
and
{b_{k}}
will closely approximate
{\eta_{k}}
and
{\theta_{k}}
, respectively, provided that
\hat{\mu}
and
\{\hat{\phi}_{j}\}
closely approximates
\mu
and
\{\phi_{j}\}
, respectively.

We assume that the canonical vectors
\{\theta_{k}\}
are group
s
-sparse, and that the associated vectors
\{b_{k}\}
are also group
s
-sparse. Let the support
S\subseteq\{1,\ldots p\}
represent the indices of non-zero elements of
\theta_{k}
or
b_{k}
, with cardinality
|S|\leq 2s
. This sparsity condition allows us to simplify equations (
S160
) and (
S163
) by replacing
X
with
X_{S}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}
, the random vector consisting of only the entries
\{X_{j}:j\in S\}
. Moreover,
\theta_{k}
and
b_{k}
can be replaced with
\theta_{k,S}
and
b_{k,S}
, respectively.

We begin by deriving an error bound for the estimation of
\psi_{k}
using
\hat{\psi}_{k}
. Since the population quantity
\psi_{k}
belongs to
L^{2}(T\mu)
and our estimate
\hat{\psi}_{k}
belongs to
L^{2}(T\hat{\mu})
, we use the parallel transport operator
\Gamma_{\mu,\hat{\mu}}
to define estimation error, as proposed in
Lin and Yao (2019)
. For ease of notation, we denote
\Gamma_{f,g}U-V
as
U\delta_{\Gamma}V\in L^{2}\left(Tg\right)
, for any vector fields
U\in L^{2}\left(Tf\right)
and
V\in L^{2}\left(Tg\right)
.

D.1
Bounding the canonical function error

Next, we derive a bound for
\hat{\psi}_{k}\delta_{\Gamma}\psi_{k}\in L^{2}(T\mu)
. Recall that, by definition,
\psi_{k}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\phi_{j}\eta_{kj}
and
\hat{\psi}_{k}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\hat{\phi}_{j}\hat{\eta}_{kj}
.

Lemma D.1
.

The following inequality holds:

\lVert\hat{\psi}_{k}\delta_{\Gamma}\psi_{k}\lVert_{\mu}^{2}\lesssim\underbrace{\left\|\hat{\eta}_{k}-a_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}\vphantom{\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}}}_{\text{Term I}}+\underbrace{\left\|a_{k}-\eta_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}\vphantom{\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}}}_{\text{Term II}}+\underbrace{\left(\left\|\eta_{k}\right\|_{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}\right)^{2}}_{\text{Term III}}.

(S164)

Remark 10
.

Term II, that is
\left\|a_{k}-\eta_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}
, captures differences between the population CCA problem described in equations (
S160
) and that in (
S163
). The non-random nature of this term complicates the analysis, as it requires deriving bounds for the expectation rather than establishing probability bounds.

The first term,
\left\|\hat{\eta}_{k}-a_{k}\right\|_{2}
, will be bounded using our multivariate CCA arguments. The second and third terms will be bounded in the following section.

D.1.1
Bounding Terms II and III of Lemma
D.1

Assuming that
\Sigma_{Y}
and
\Sigma_{Z}
are invertible, and from the definitions of
a_{k}
and
\eta_{k}
as the solutions to the problems in equations (
S163
) and (
S160
) respectively, we have that

\displaystyle a_{k}=\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2}\tilde{a}_{k},

(S165)

\displaystyle\eta_{k}=\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\tilde{\eta}_{k},

(S166)

where
\tilde{a}_{k}
is the
k
th eigenvector (unit vector) of
A^{\top}A
and
\tilde{\eta}
is the
k
th eigenvector (unit vector) of
C^{\top}C
, where we have

\displaystyle A=\Sigma_{X_{S}}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{{X_{S}}Z}\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2},

(S167)

\displaystyle C=\Sigma_{X_{S}}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{{X_{S}}Y}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}.

(S168)

Applying inequality
17
in Section
F
, we have that

\left\|a_{k}-\eta_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\left\|\tilde{a}_{k}-\tilde{\eta}_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

(S169)

Noting that
A^{\top}A
has the same eigenvectors as
\left|A\right|
, where
\left|A\right|\equiv\left(A^{\top}A\right)^{1/2}
, we can apply the Davis-Kahan theorem (Corollary 3 of
Yu et al. (2015)
) and obtain

\left\|\tilde{a}_{k}-\tilde{\eta}_{k}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\left\|\left|A\right|-\left|C\right|\right\|_{2}}{\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}-\gamma_{k},\gamma_{k}-\gamma_{k+1}\right)},\qquad k=1,\ldots K,

(S170)

where we assume
\tilde{a}_{k}^{\top}\tilde{\eta}_{k}\geq 0
and we use the conventions
\gamma_{K+1}=-\infty
, and
\gamma_{0}=\infty
.
Next, note that

\left\|\left|A\right|-\left|C\right|\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\left|A\right|-\left|C\right|\right\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{2}\left\|A-C\right\|_{F},

(S171)

where the second inequality is identity
19
from Section
F
. We can then bound
\left\|A-C\right\|_{F}^{2}
in terms of
\left\|\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}^{2}
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Y-Z\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]
using the following lemma.

Lemma D.2
.

Under the group
s
-sparsity assumptions on
b_{k}
and
\theta_{k}
, we have that

\left\|A-C\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq 2s\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\left\|\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Y-Z\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\right].

(S172)

Next, we bound
\left\|\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}^{2}
in terms of
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Y-Z\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]
using the following lemma.

Lemma D.3
.

It can be shown that

\left\|\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\operatorname{max}\left(\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2},\left\|\Sigma_{Z}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\right)^{3}\operatorname{max}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z\right\|_{2}^{2}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\right).

(S173)

Additionally, we have that

\left\|\Sigma_{Y}-\Sigma_{Z}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\operatorname{max}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z\right\|_{2}^{2}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right].

(S174)

Combining the results of this section we obtain the following bound on
\left\|a_{k}-\eta_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}
in terms of
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]
.

Lemma D.4
.

Under the group
s
-sparsity assumptions on
b_{k}
and
\theta_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|a_{k}-\eta_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\left(1+\frac{s\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]}{\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}-\gamma_{k},\gamma_{k}-\gamma_{k+1}\right)^{2}}\right)\operatorname{max}\left(\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2},\left\|\Sigma_{Z}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\right)^{3}\operatorname{max}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z\right\|_{2}^{2}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{s\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}-\gamma_{k},\gamma_{k}-\gamma_{k+1}\right)^{2}}\Bigg]

Additionally, if we assume that
\left\|\Sigma_{Z}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}
,
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]
, and
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2},\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\geq 1
, then the statement simplifies as follows:

\left\|a_{k}-\eta_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\frac{s\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}^{4}E\left[\left\|Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]^{2}}{\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}-\gamma_{k},\gamma_{k}-\gamma_{k+1}\right)^{2}}.

(S175)

Remark 11
.

In subsequent discussions, we will detail the conditions necessary for the additional assumptions stated here to hold.

Having established a bound for
\left\|a_{k}-\eta_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}
in terms of
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]
, we now turn our attention to bounding
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]
using ’known’ quantities. In the process, we will derive a bound for
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}\right]
, which will enable us to derive a probabilistic bound. This, in turn, will be used to bound Term III in Lemma
D.1
.

To establish a bound for
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]
, we first begin by introducing a lemma to bound
\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}
.

Lemma D.5
.

It can be shown that

\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2d\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{i}\delta_{\Gamma}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}+2\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}.

(S176)

Next, we aim to establish a bound for
\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}
. Consider an operator
\hat{\mathcal{C}}
on
L^{2}(T\hat{\mu})
. We use parallel transport to define
\Phi\hat{\mathcal{C}}
as the operator on
L^{2}(T\mu)
such that
\Phi\hat{\mathcal{C}}\left(V\right)=\Gamma_{\hat{\mu},\mu}\left(\hat{\mathcal{C}}\left(\Gamma_{{\mu},\hat{\mu}}V\right)\right)\in L^{2}(T\mu)
, for every
V\in L^{2}(T\mu)
. We also define the operator
\hat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mu}\equiv\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\otimes\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}
on
L^{2}(T\mu)
. Moreover, we use
\lVert\cdot\lVert_{\operatorname{op}}
to denote the operator norm on
L^{2}(T\mu)
.

Lemma D.6
.

For any
j\geq 1
, we have that

\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\lVert\mathcal{C}-\hat{\mathcal{C}}\lVert_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}+\lVert\hat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mu}-\Phi\hat{\mathcal{C}}\lVert_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}}{\operatorname{min}\left(\omega_{j-1}-\omega_{j},\omega_{j}-\omega_{j+1}\right)^{2}}.

(S177)

We introduce the following lemma to bound the expectation of the terms in the previous lemma.

Lemma D.7
.

If
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]<\infty
, then

\mathbb{E}\left[\lVert\mathcal{C}-\hat{\mathcal{C}}\lVert_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}\right]\leq\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right].

(S178)

Additionally, under the assumption that
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{i}\delta_{\Gamma}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]^{1/2}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{i}\delta_{\Gamma}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\right]
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\lVert\hat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mu}-\Phi\hat{\mathcal{C}}\lVert_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}\right]\lesssim\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{i}\delta_{\Gamma}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\right]\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{i}\delta_{\Gamma}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\right].

(S179)

The lemma above shows that, in order to bound
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\right]
, it is first necessary to bound
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{i}\delta_{\Gamma}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\right]
. To do this, we need the following more technical results. But first, we state some preliminary definitions. Let
T\mathcal{M}
denote the tangent bundle of
\mathcal{M}
, and let
\nabla
denote the Riemannian connection on
\mathcal{M}
. The next result, which is a mean value theorem for the parallel transport operation, is used in the proof of Lemma
D.9
.

Lemma D.8
.

For a smooth vector field
U:\mathcal{M}\rightarrow T\mathcal{M}
,
x,y\in\mathcal{M}
, with minimizing geodesic
\gamma(t)
between
x
and
y
(so that
\gamma(0)=x
and
\gamma(d(x,y))=y
), we have that

\lVert\mathcal{P}_{y,x}U(y)-U(x)\lVert_{x}\leq d(y,x)\underset{c\in\left[0,d(x,y)\right]}{\operatorname{sup}\ }\lVert\nabla_{\gamma^{\prime}(c)}U(\gamma(c))\lVert_{\gamma(c)}.

(S180)

Remark 12
.

When the vector field
U
has bounded Hessian
H
, defined below, we can use this result to bound the parallel transport error by the geodesic distance
d(x,y)
between the base points of the vector field.

For
x\in\mathcal{M}
and
t\in\mathcal{T}
, define
f_{t}(x)\equiv\frac{1}{2}d^{2}\left(x,y_{1}(t)\right)
. Let
H_{t}
be the Riemannian Hessian of
f_{t}
, i.e.
H_{t}(x):T_{x}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow T_{x}\mathcal{M}
such that for all
x\in\mathcal{M}
,
t\in\mathcal{T}
, and
v\in T_{x}\mathcal{M}
,
H_{t}(x)(v)=\nabla_{v}\operatorname{grad}f_{t}(x)
. For a mapping
A(x)
, which for each
x
is an operator
A(x):T_{x}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow T_{x}\mathcal{M}
, we define the operator norm at
x
of
A
:
\lVert A\lVert_{\operatorname{op},x}\equiv\underset{v\in T_{x}\mathcal{M},\lVert v\lVert_{x}=1}{\operatorname{sup}\ }\lVert A(x)(v)\lVert_{x}
. Recall that
\tau=|\mathcal{T}|
is the length of the time interval of the functional data.

Lemma D.9
.

Assume that

1.

\mathcal{M}
is a complete, simply-connected Riemannian manifold with nonpositive sectional curvature.

2.

\underset{t\in\mathcal{T}}{\operatorname{sup}\ }\mathbb{E}\left[d\left(y_{1}(t),y_{2}(t)\right)^{3}\right]<\infty
.

3.

\underset{t\in\mathcal{T},x\in\mathcal{M}}{\operatorname{sup}\ }\lVert H_{t}(x)\lVert_{\operatorname{op},x}^{2}\lesssim 1
with probability
1
.

Then,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{i}\delta_{\Gamma}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{\tau}{N}.

(S181)

The next lemma combines the results of Lemmas
D.6
,
D.7
, and
D.9
.

Lemma D.10
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
D.9
, and additionally assuming that

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{i}\delta_{\Gamma}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]^{1/2}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{i}\delta_{\Gamma}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\right]
, and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]\geq 1
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{N}\frac{\tau\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]}{\operatorname{min}\left(\omega_{j-1}-\omega_{j},\omega_{j}-\omega_{j+1}\right)^{2}}.

(S182)

From Lemma
D.10
and Markov’s inequality, we obtain the following inequality, which we can use to bound Term III in Lemma
D.1
.

Corollary D.1
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
D.10
, we have that

\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{N}\frac{\tau\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]}{\operatorname{min}\left(\omega_{j-1}-\omega_{j},\omega_{j}-\omega_{j+1}\right)^{2}}\right).

(S183)

Now we can combine the results of Lemmas
D.5
,
D.9
and
D.10
to obtain a bound on
\mathbb{E}\left[\lVert Z-Y\lVert^{2}\right]
.

Lemma D.11
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
D.10
and additionally assuming that

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]^{1/2}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\right]
, we have that

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{\tau d}{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]^{3/2}\underset{j=1,\ldots d}{\operatorname{max}}\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{j}-\omega_{j+1}}\right)^{2}.

(S184)

The proof follows from applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and collecting similar terms.

Now we can state the conditions under which the additional assumptions of Lemma
D.4
will hold. From now on, we keep tracking the terms
\tau
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]
in the error bounds, but we assume they are constant.

Lemma D.12
.

We have that

\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}=\omega_{d}^{-1}
,
\left\|\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2}=\omega_{1}
, and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\omega_{j}
. Additionally, if
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right],\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\geq 1
, and

\frac{d}{N}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{d}\omega_{j}}{\omega_{d}^{2}}\underset{j=1,\ldots d}{\operatorname{max}}\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{j}-\omega_{j+1}}\right)^{2}=o(1),

(S185)

then
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]=o(1)
,
\left\|\Sigma_{Z}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}
,
\left\|\Sigma_{Z}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2}
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]
.

Now we can combine Lemmas
D.4
,
D.11
, and
D.12
to obtain a final bound on
\left\|a_{k}-\eta_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}
.

Theorem D.1
.

Under the assumptions of Lemmas
D.11
and
D.12
, we have that

\left\|a_{k}-\eta_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\tau sd}{N}\underset{j=1,\ldots d}{\operatorname{max}}\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{j}-\omega_{j+1}}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{d}\omega_{j}}{\omega_{d}^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]^{3/2}}{\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}-\gamma_{k},\gamma_{k}-\gamma_{k+1}\right)^{2}}.

(S186)

Before we combine the bounds for the three components detailed in Lemma
D.1
, we show that
\tilde{\gamma}_{k}
and
\gamma_{k}
are asymptotically equivalent, which allows us to simplify the expression of our final bounds. Recall that the correlations
\{\tilde{\gamma}_{k}\}
are defined using equation (
S163
) as the canonical correlations between
X
and
Z
, while the correlations
\{\gamma_{k}\}
are the canonical correlations between
X
and
Y
. To this end, we first state the following bound, which follows directly from Lemmas
D.2
and
D.3
. This will also be used later to derive a bound for
\left\|\theta_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}
.

Lemma D.13
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
D.12
, we have that

\left\|A-C\right\|_{F}^{2}\lesssim s\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}^{3}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right].

(S187)

We can prove the next lemma by using Lemmas
D.11
,
D.12
, and
D.13
.

Lemma D.14
.

Under the assumptions of Lemmas
D.11
and
D.12
, and further assuming that
\tau
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]
are absolute constants, that the canonical correlations
\{\gamma_{k}\}
and
\{\tilde{\gamma}_{k}\}
are bounded from below and that

\frac{ds}{N}\frac{\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\omega_{j}\right)^{2}}{\omega_{d}^{3}}\underset{j=1,\ldots d}{\operatorname{max}}\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{j}-\omega_{j+1}}\right)^{2}=o(1),

(S188)

then
\gamma_{k}
and
\tilde{\gamma}_{k}
are asymptotically equivalent, i.e.,
\gamma_{k}\lesssim\tilde{\gamma}_{k}
and
\tilde{\gamma}_{k}\lesssim\gamma_{k}
. Additionally,
\gamma_{k}^{2}\lesssim\tilde{\gamma}_{k}^{2}
and
\tilde{\gamma}_{k}^{2}\lesssim\gamma_{k}^{2}
.

Now we are now in a position to establish bounds for all three terms in Lemma
D.1
, using Theorem
C.3
(applied to
X
and
Z
and using the bound for
\eta
), Theorem
D.1
, and Corollary
D.1
for Term I, II, and III, respectively. This will yield our final bound for the canonical functions
\lVert\hat{\psi}_{k}\delta_{\Gamma}\psi_{k}\lVert_{\mu}^{2}
, which is presented in Section
D.3
.

D.2
Bounding high-dimensional canonical vector error

In this section, we derive a bound for the estimation error of the high-dimensional canonical vectors,
\left\|\theta_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}
. Having already derived a bound for the canonical functions, the proof is straightforward. We start with an application of the triangle inequality. For
k=1,\ldots K
,

\left\|\theta_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\left\|\theta_{k}-b_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|b_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2},

(S189)

where
b_{k}
is the high-dimensional canonical vector given by the solution to problem (
S163
). Assuming
s
-group sparsity for
\theta_{k}
and
b_{k}
, we represent the associated vectors with non-zero entries as
\theta_{k,S}
and
b_{k,S}
. Recall that these are at most
2s
-dimensional. By definition, we then have
\left\|\theta_{k}-b_{k}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\theta_{k,S}-b_{k,S}\right\|_{2}
, hence

\left\|\theta_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\left\|\theta_{k,S}-b_{k,S}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|b_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

(S190)

To bound the second term, we can use Theorem
C.3
, applied to the random vectors
X
and
Z
. To bound the second term, we can make the following argument, which is similar to the one made to bound
\left\|\eta_{k}-a_{k}\right\|_{2}
. Let

\displaystyle A=\Sigma_{X_{S}}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{{X_{S}}Z}\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2},

(S191)

\displaystyle C=\Sigma_{X_{S}}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{{X_{S}}Y}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}.

(S192)

Then, from classical CCA
(
Uurtio et al., 2018
)
,
we have that
\theta_{k,S}=\Sigma_{X_{S}}^{-1/2}\tilde{\theta}_{k,S}
, where
\tilde{\theta}_{k,S}
is the
k
th eigenvector of
CC^{\top}
, and
b_{k}=\Sigma_{X_{S}}^{-1/2}\tilde{b}_{k,S}
, where
\tilde{b}_{k,S}
is the
k
th eigenvector of
AA^{\top}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\|\theta_{k,S}-b_{k,S}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\Sigma_{X_{S}}^{-1/2}\tilde{\theta}_{k,S}-\Sigma_{X_{S}}^{-1/2}\tilde{b}_{k,S}\right\|_{2}

(S193)

\displaystyle=\left\|\Sigma_{X_{S}}^{-1/2}\left(\tilde{\theta}_{k,S}-\tilde{b}_{k,S}\right)\right\|_{2}

(S194)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\Sigma_{X_{S}}^{-1/2}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\tilde{\theta}_{k,S}-\tilde{b}_{k,S}\right\|_{2}.

(S195)

Since
A^{\top}A
has the same eigenvectors as
\left|A\right|=\left(AA^{\top}\right)^{1/2}
, we can then use the Davis-Kahan theorem
(
Yu et al., 2015
)
to derive the following bound. If
\tilde{\theta}_{k,S}^{\top}\tilde{b}_{k,S}\geq 0
, then

\left\|\tilde{\theta}_{k,S}-\tilde{b}_{k,S}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\left\|\left|A^{\top}\right|-\left|C^{\top}\right|\right\|_{2}}{\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}-\gamma_{k},\gamma_{k}-\gamma_{k+1}\right)}.

(S196)

The term
\left\|\left|A^{\top}\right|-\left|C^{\top}\right|\right\|_{2}
can be bounded as follows:

\left\|\left|A^{\top}\right|-\left|C^{\top}\right|\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\left|A^{\top}\right|-\left|C^{\top}\right|\right\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{2}\left\|A^{\top}-C^{\top}\right\|_{F}=\sqrt{2}\left\|A-C\right\|_{F},

(S197)

where the second inequality is identity
19
from Section
F
. Having established a bound for
\left\|\tilde{\theta}_{k,S}-\tilde{b}_{k,S}\right\|_{2}
in terms of
\left\|A-C\right\|_{F}
, we can apply similar arguments to those used to bound
\left\|a_{k}-\eta_{k}\right\|_{2}
in order to derive a bound for
\left\|\tilde{\theta}_{k,S}-\tilde{b}_{k,S}\right\|_{2}
in terms of
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]
. Combining the results of this section, using Lemmas
D.11
and
D.13
, we establish the following result.

Lemma D.15
.

Under the assumptions of Lemmas
D.11
and
D.12
, and if
\tilde{\theta}_{k,S}^{\top}\tilde{b}_{k,S}\geq 0
, we have that

\left\|\theta_{k,S}-b_{k,S}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\left\|{\Sigma_{X_{S}}^{-1/2}}\right\|_{2}^{2}s\left\|\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\right\|_{2}^{3}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]^{2}}{{\operatorname{min}\left(\gamma_{k-1}-\gamma_{k},\gamma_{k}-\gamma_{k+1}\right)^{2}}}\frac{\tau d}{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]^{3/2}\underset{j=1,\ldots d}{\operatorname{max}}\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{j}-\omega_{j+1}}\right)^{2}.

(S198)

Next, by applying Theorem
C.3
, Lemma
D.15
, and equation (
S190
), we can establish the final bound for the high-dimensional canonical vector error
\left\|\theta_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}
, which is presented in Section
D.3
.

D.3
Final rates

In this section, we present our final results. Recall that we denote as
K=\operatorname{max}\left\{i\in\{1,\ldots{d^{(\text{corr})}}\}:\gamma_{i}>0\right\}
the number of nontrivial canonical vectors and we use the conventions
\gamma_{K+1}^{2}=-\infty
and
\gamma_{0}^{2}=+\infty
. The random vector
Z
was defined in equation (
S162
) and represents a ‘sample’ version of
Y
where
\phi_{j}
and
\mu
are replaced by their finite-sample estimates.

For clarity, we first provide a comprehensive list of our assumptions. For the definitions of the quantities that appear below, please see the beginning of Section
D
.

Assumption D.1

(Manifold Properties)
.

1.

The manifold
\mathcal{M}
is a complete simply-connected Riemannian manifold with nonpositive sectional curvature.

2.

The curvature
\underset{t\in\mathcal{T},x\in\mathcal{M}}{\operatorname{sup}\ }\lVert H_{t}(x)\lVert_{\operatorname{op},x}^{2}
is bounded with probability
1
.

Assumption D.2

(Distributional Assumptions)
.

1.

The random vectors
X
and
Z
are strict sub-Gaussian random vectors (Definition
C.2
).

2.

The covariance matrices
\Sigma_{X}
,
\Sigma_{Y}
, and
\Sigma_{Z}
are invertible.

3.

The group
s
-sparsity assumption holds for
\{b_{k}\}
and
\{\theta_{k}\}
.

4.

The matrix
\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}
satisfies the group restricted eigenvalue condition RE
(s,3,d)
(Definition
C.3
) with parameter
\kappa=\kappa(s,d,\Sigma_{X}^{1/2})
.

5.

The functional data are such that
\underset{t\in\mathcal{T}}{\operatorname{sup}\ }\mathbb{E}\left[d\left(y_{1}(t),y_{2}(t)\right)^{3}\right]<\infty
.

Assumption D.3

(Rate Assumptions)
.

1.

There exists a complete orthonormal system for
L^{2}(T\mu)
,
\{\phi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
, such that a
{d^{(\text{corr})}}
-dimensional Assumption
1.1
holds with
p\geq d\geq{d^{(\text{corr})}}
, where
d
is the chosen number of principal components in Algorithm
1
;

2.

\operatorname{cond}\left(\Sigma_{Y}\right)^{2}d=o(N)
;

3.

\kappa^{2}s^{2}d\log(p)=o(N)
;

4.

ds\frac{\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\omega_{j}\right)^{2}}{\omega_{d}^{3}}\underset{j=1,\ldots d}{\operatorname{max}}\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{j}-\omega_{j+1}}\right)^{2}=o(N)
;

5.

The correlations
\gamma_{1},\ldots\gamma_{K}
are bounded from below and distinct from one another, as well as
\tilde{\gamma}_{1},\ldots\tilde{\gamma}_{K}
.

6.

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]^{1/2}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\right]
;

7.

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{i}\delta_{\Gamma}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]^{1/2}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{i}\delta_{\Gamma}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\right]
.

Assumption D.4

(Minor Assumptions)
.

1.

The quantities
\left\|\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{2,\infty},\left\|T\right\|_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}
are bounded from above and are
\geq 1
.

2.

The variables
\tau
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]
are constants.

3.

The following quantities are larger than 1:
\kappa,\omega_{1},\omega_{d}^{-1},\left\|\eta\right\|_{\infty},\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{-1}\right\|_{2},\left\|\Sigma_{Z}^{-1}\right\|_{2}
.

4.

a_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{Z}^{1/2}\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}\eta_{k}\geq 0
and
\hat{\eta}_{k}^{\top}\hat{\Sigma_{Z}}^{1/2}\Sigma_{Z}^{1/2}a_{k}\geq 0
for
k=1,\ldots K
.

Next, we present our main results.

Theorem D.2

(Canonical Function Error Bound)
.

Under Assumptions
D.1
-
D.4
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{\psi}_{k}\delta_{\Gamma}\psi_{k}\lVert_{\mu}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\frac{d^{2}s\log(p)}{N}\right)\frac{\tau}{\min_{j\neq k}\min\left\{\left|{\gamma_{k}}^{2}-{\gamma_{j}}^{2}\right|,\left|{\gamma_{k}}-{\gamma_{j}}\right|\right\}^{2}}

(S199)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{d}\omega_{j}}{\omega_{d}^{2}}\right)^{2}\underset{j=1,\ldots d}{\operatorname{max}}\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{j}-\omega_{j+1}}\right)^{2}\kappa\left\|\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\eta\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]^{3/2},

(S200)

with
k=1,\ldots K
.

Theorem D.3

(Canonical Vector Error Bound)
.

Under Assumptions
D.1
-
D.4
, and additionally assuming that
\theta_{k,S}^{\top}\Sigma_{X_{S}}b_{k,S}\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\theta_{k}-\hat{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\frac{ds\log(p)}{N}\right)\frac{\tau}{\min_{j\neq k}\min\left\{\left|{\gamma_{k}}^{2}-{\gamma_{j}}^{2}\right|,\left|{\gamma_{k}}-{\gamma_{j}}\right|\right\}^{2}}

(S201)

\displaystyle\cdot\left[\frac{\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\omega_{j}\right)^{2}}{\omega_{d}^{3}}\underset{j=1,\ldots d}{\operatorname{max}}\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{j}-\omega_{j+1}}\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]^{3/2}\left\|\Sigma_{X_{S}}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}+\left(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\gamma_{k}}\right)^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{X}\right\|_{2,\infty}\kappa^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\right],

(S202)

with
k=1,\ldots K
.

Here we make a few remarks on the assumptions of Theorems
D.2
and
D.3
. Some of the more technical assumptions arise from avoiding overly simplifying assumptions. For instance, with the exception of the curvature-related quantity
H_{t}(x)
, we do not assume that the random variables/functions are bounded. Furthermore, we avoid assuming Gaussianity of the random variables of interest and instead assume these are sub-Gaussians. As in
Lin and Yao (2019)
, we do not assume that the Frechét mean
\mu
is known and instead estimate it using its sample version
\hat{\mu}
; this choice introduces significant complexity, making it necessary to use the parallel transport operator to compare estimates and estimands, which are defined in different tangent spaces. These challenges are further compounded by the dimensionality reduction step that takes place before CCA, which requires that we derive bounds in
expectation
rather than in probability. Specifically, this requires showing that
\mathbb{E}\left[d\left(\hat{\mu}(t),\mu(t)\right)^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{N}
, i.e., equation (
S253
),
using the results of
Schötz (2019)
as opposed to those of
Lin and Yao (2019)
. See also Remark
10
.

Remarks on Assumption
D.1
:

Assumption
D.1
is required to bound the term
\mathbb{E}\left[d\left(\hat{\mu}(t),\mu(t)\right)^{2}\right]
. See also Lemma
D.9
. Here,
H_{t}(x)
is the Riemannian Hessian of the random function
d^{2}(x,y(t))
, and is related to curvature on the manifold
(
Pennec, 2017
)
. Assuming this quantity is bounded allows us to bound the parallel transport distance in terms of the geodesic distance, as shown in Lemma
D.8
.

Remarks on Assumption
D.2
:

Items
1
-
4
of Assumption
D.2
are used to facilitate the application of our multivariate CCA results in Section
3.1
to the sparse-functional setting considered here. Specifically, Items
3
-
4
are used in particular to get fast-rate bounds, which match the root-n estimation rate of the functional quantities. We note that in item
3
, we do not require that
\theta_{k}
and
b_{k}
have the same sparsity structure, but only that they are both
s
-sparse.
Item
4
is a generalization of the standard restricted-eigenvalue condition in Lasso theory
(
Hastie et al., 2015
)
and is equivalent to the one proposed in
Gaynanova (2020)
. Item
5
is a weak assumption about the boundedness of the variance of
y(t)
on the manifold and along with Assumption
D.1
is necessary to show that
\mathbb{E}\left[d\left(\hat{\mu}(t),\mu(t)\right)^{2}\right]
is root-
n
consistent.

Remarks on Assumption
D.3
:

Item
1
of Assumption
D.3
, that the correlation with the high-dimensional data is captured in a finite-dimensional subspace of the functional data, is necessary to even define the canonical directions for functional data, as stated in the main manuscript. The condition that
d\leq p
formalizes our asymmetrical treatment of the data, and allows for the CCA problem to become a sparse regression problem.
d\geq{d^{(\text{corr})}}
is necessary to ensure we capture all of the components of
\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y
which are correlated with
X
. Items
2
-
5
are mainly used to simplify the theorem statements by allowing us to bound norms of estimated quantities using the corresponding population quantities. In particular, Item
2
is used in conjunction with Lemma
D.12
to show that
\left\|\hat{\Sigma}_{Z}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{Y}\right\|_{2}
. Item
3
allows us to only make a group restricted eigenvalue assumption on
\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}
rather than the data matrix
\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\mathbb{X}
(see Lemma
C.9
). Item
4
states that the variances
\omega_{j}
of the principal scores
Y_{j}
should not shrink too quickly as
N
and
d
grow. Note that if
d
is assumed constant, the condition reduces to
s=o(N)
. This is used to establish the simplifying bounds given in Lemmas
D.12
and
D.14
. Item
5
is used to replace
\hat{\gamma}_{k}
and
\tilde{\gamma}_{k}
with the population canonical correlations
\gamma_{k}
(see Lemma
D.14
and the discussion in the proof of Theorem
C.2
). Items
6
and
7
are technical conditions. These mainly arise from the complexity of the setting considered here. The assumption
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]^{1/2}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\right]
could be replaced with a boundness assumption on
\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}
, or alternatively, by adopting a sample splitting strategy to estimate
\hat{\mu}
, the principal components
\{\hat{\phi}_{j}\}
, and to carry out CCA. This can be seen from the second term of equation (
S176
), where the absence of one of these assumptions requires that we use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, introducing fourth moments. Note that if we were to assume that
\hat{\mu}=\mu
, then
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{i}\delta_{\Gamma}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{i}\right\|_{\mu}^{4}\right]^{1/2}=0
, immediately satisfying the condition in item 7.

Remarks on Assumption
D.4
:

Items
1
-
3
are not critical and only serve the purpose of simplifying the theorem statements. Item
4
is introduced to account for the sign ambiguity of the CCA solutions.

D.4
Proofs for results in Section
D

Proof of Lemma
D.1
:

We define
\tilde{\psi}_{k}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\hat{\phi}_{j}a_{kj}
. For ease of notation, we drop the
k
in writing
\hat{\psi}_{k}
,
\psi_{k}
and
\tilde{\psi}_{k}
,
\eta_{k}
, etc.
We have

\lVert\hat{\psi}\delta_{\Gamma}\psi\lVert_{\mu}^{2}=\left\|\Gamma_{\hat{\mu},\mu}\hat{\psi}-\psi\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\leq 2\left\|\Gamma_{\hat{\mu},\mu}\hat{\psi}-\Gamma_{\hat{\mu},\mu}\tilde{\psi}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}+2\left\|\Gamma_{\hat{\mu},\mu}\tilde{\psi}-\psi\right\|_{\mu}^{2}.

(S203)

The first term in equation (
S203
) is

2\left\|\Gamma_{\hat{\mu},\mu}\left(\hat{\psi}-\tilde{\psi}\right)\right\|_{\mu}^{2}=\left\|\Gamma_{\mu,\hat{\mu}}\left(\Gamma_{\hat{\mu},\mu}\left(\hat{\psi}-\tilde{\psi}\right)\right)\right\|_{\hat{\mu}}^{2}=2\left\|\hat{\psi}-\tilde{\psi}\right\|_{\hat{\mu}}^{2}.

Define
\bar{\psi}
as
\sum_{j=1}^{d}\hat{\phi}_{j}\eta_{j}
.
Then the second term in equation (
S203
) is

\leq 4\left\|\Gamma_{\hat{\mu},\mu}\tilde{\psi}-\Gamma_{\hat{\mu},\mu}\bar{\psi}\right\|_{\mu}^{2}+4\left\|\Gamma_{\hat{\mu},\mu}\bar{\psi}-\psi\right\|_{\mu}^{2}.

Therefore,

\lVert\hat{\psi}\delta_{\Gamma}\psi\lVert_{\mu}^{2}\lesssim\left\|\hat{\psi}-\tilde{\psi}\right\|_{\hat{\mu}}^{2}+\left\|\tilde{\psi}-\bar{\psi}\right\|_{\hat{\mu}}^{2}+\left\|\bar{\psi}\delta_{\Gamma}\psi\right\|_{\mu}^{2}.

(S204)

The first term in equation (
S204
) is

\left\|\hat{\psi}-\tilde{\psi}\right\|_{\hat{\mu}}^{2}=\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\hat{\phi}_{j}\hat{\eta}_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{d}a_{j}\hat{\phi}_{j}\right\|_{\hat{\mu}}^{2}=\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\hat{\phi}_{j}\left(\hat{\eta}_{j}-a_{j}\right)\right\|_{\hat{\mu}}^{2}=\left\|\hat{\eta}-a\right\|_{2}^{2},

(S205)

where in the third equality we have used that the
\hat{\phi}_{j}
are orthonormal in
L^{2}(T\hat{\mu})
.
Similarly, the second term is

\left\|\tilde{\psi}-\bar{\psi}\right\|_{\hat{\mu}}^{2}=\left\|a-\eta\right\|_{2}^{2}.

(S206)

By the triangle inequality, the third term is

\left\|\bar{\psi}\delta_{\Gamma}\psi\right\|_{\mu}^{2}\leq\left(\left\|\eta\right\|_{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}\right)^{2},

(S207)

completing the proof. ∎

Proof of Lemma
D.2
:

For ease of notation, we write
X_{S}
as
X
throughout the proof of the lemma. From the definition of
A
and
C
, we have

\left\|A-C\right\|_{F}=\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{{X}Z}\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2}-\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{{X}Y}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right\|_{F}=\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[XZ^{\top}\right]\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2}-\mathbb{E}\left[XY^{\top}\right]\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\right\|_{F}.

(S208)

From the linearity of expectation, this is

=\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}X\left(Z^{\top}\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2}-Y^{\top}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}\right)\right]\right\|_{F}=\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}X\left(\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2}Z-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y\right)^{\top}\right]\right\|_{F}.

(S209)

By Theorem 2.6.7 of
Hsing and Eubank (2015)
, using the Frobenious norm of an outer product, and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|A-C\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}X\left(\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2}Z-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y\right)^{\top}\right\|_{F}\right]

(S210)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}X\right\|_{2}\left\|\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2}Z-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y\right\|_{2}\right]

(S211)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}X\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2}Z-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

(S212)

We have that
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}X\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\operatorname{tr}\left(\Sigma_{X}^{-1}XX^{\top}\right)\right]=\operatorname{tr}\left(I_{s}\right)=s
. Using this along with identity
17
from Section
F
to upper bound
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma_{Z}^{-1/2}Z-\Sigma_{Y}^{-1/2}Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]^{1/2}
completes the proof.

Proof of Lemma
D.3
:

The first statement follows from the second statement, by identity
14
from Section
F
and Lemma
D.3
. To show the second statement, we begin with

\left\|\Sigma_{Y}-\Sigma_{Z}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[YY^{\top}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[ZZ^{\top}\right]\right\|_{2}=\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[YY^{\top}-ZZ^{\top}\right]\right\|_{2},

(S213)

and using Theorem 2.6.7 of
Hsing and Eubank (2015)
, this is

\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|YY^{\top}-ZZ^{\top}\right\|_{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|YY^{\top}-ZY^{\top}+ZY^{\top}-ZZ^{\top}\right\|_{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z\left(Z-Y\right)^{\top}+\left(Z-Y\right)Y^{\top}\right\|_{2}\right].

(S214)

From this, the triangle inequality, and using the two-norm of an outer product, we have

\left\|\Sigma_{Y}-\Sigma_{Z}\right\|_{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z\left(Z-Y\right)^{\top}\right\|_{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(Z-Y\right)Y^{\top}\right\|_{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z\right\|_{2}\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}\left\|Y\right\|_{2}\right]

(S215)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the right hand side is

=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|Z\right\|_{2}+\left\|Y\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|Z\right\|_{2}+\left\|Y\right\|_{2}\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]^{1/2}

(S216)

The second statement of the lemma follows, and the proof is complete. ∎

Proof of Lemma
D.5
:

Define
W\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
as the random vector with
W_{j}\equiv\llangle\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1},\Gamma_{\hat{\mu},\mu}\hat{\phi}_{j}\rrangle_{\mu}
for
j=1,\ldots d
. Then

\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\left\|Z-W\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\left\|W-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}.

(S217)

We have

\displaystyle Z_{j}-W_{j}

\displaystyle=\llangle\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{1},\hat{\phi}_{j}\rrangle_{\hat{\mu}}-\llangle\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1},\Gamma_{\hat{\mu},\mu}\hat{\phi}_{j}\rrangle_{\mu}

(S218)

\displaystyle=\llangle\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{1},\hat{\phi}_{j}\rrangle_{\hat{\mu}}-\llangle\Gamma_{\mu,\hat{\mu}}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1},\hat{\phi}_{j}\rrangle_{\hat{\mu}}

(S219)

\displaystyle=\llangle\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{1}-\Gamma_{\mu,\hat{\mu}}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1},\hat{\phi}_{j}\rrangle_{\hat{\mu}},

(S220)

and therefore, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and because the
\hat{\phi}_{j}
are orthonormal along
\hat{\mu}
,

\displaystyle\left|Z_{j}-W_{j}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{1}-\Gamma_{\mu,\hat{\mu}}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\lVert_{\hat{\mu}}\lVert\hat{\phi}_{j}\lVert_{\hat{\mu}}

(S221)

\displaystyle=\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\delta_{\Gamma}\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{1}\right\|_{\hat{\mu}}

(S222)

\displaystyle=\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\hat{\mu}}y_{1}\delta_{\Gamma}\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\right\|_{\mu},

(S223)

where in the last equality we have used that
\lVert U\delta_{\Gamma}V\lVert_{\mu}=\lVert V\delta_{\Gamma}U\lVert_{\hat{\mu}}
for
U\in L^{2}(T\hat{\mu})
and
V\in L^{2}(T\mu)
. We also have

\displaystyle W_{j}-Y_{j}

\displaystyle=\llangle\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1},\Gamma_{\hat{\mu},\mu}\hat{\phi}_{j}\rrangle_{\mu}-\llangle\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1},\phi_{j}\rrangle_{\mu}

(S224)

\displaystyle=\llangle\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1},\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\rrangle_{\mu},

(S225)

so that again by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\left|W_{j}-Y_{j}\right|\leq\left\|\operatorname{Log}_{\mu}y_{1}\right\|_{\mu}\left\|\hat{\phi}_{j}\delta_{\Gamma}\phi_{j}\right\|_{\mu}.

(S226)

Thus,

\displaystyle\left\|Z-Y\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left|Z_{j}-W_{j}\right|^{2}+\left|W_{j}-Y_{j}\right|^{2}

(S227)
